<!-- page 363 -->
INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT FOR COURT COLLECTION AND 
CRIMINAL JUSTICE SERVICES 
 
 THIS INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT (“IGA”) is made as o f 
_________________, 2025 (the “Effective Date”) by and between NA V AJO COUNTY, a political 
subdivision of the State of Arizona (the “County”), and the incorporated Arizona Municipalities of 
HOLBROOK, PINETOP -LAKESIDE, SHOW LOW, SNOWFLAKE, TAYLOR, AND 
WINSLOW (individually the “Municipality” and collectively the “Municipalities”).  
 
WHEREAS, the County operates the Holbrook, Pinetop-Lakeside, Show Low, Snowflake-Taylor, 
and Winslow Justice Courts in the respective Municipalities; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Municipalities operate their own municipal Magistrate Courts; and  
 
WHEREAS, both types of courts process cases involving matters originating within the corporate 
limits of the respective Municipality; and 
 
WHEREAS, the court collection duties of the Justice Court and the Magistrate Court are similar 
in most respects; and 
 
WHEREAS, A.R.S. §§ 11-951 and 11-952 authorize public agencies such as the County and the 
Municipalities to enter into intergovernmental agreements for joint or cooperative action; and 
 
WHEREAS, A.R.S. § 11- 952(L) specifically provides that public agencies may enter into 
intergovernmental agreements with the Superior Court, Justice Courts, and Magistrate Courts 
concerning related services and facilities for a term not to exceed ten years, with the approval  of 
any such agreement by the Presiding Judge of the Superior Court in the county in which the court 
or courts providing the services or facilities are located; and 
 
WHEREAS, each Municipality has requested that the County Court Collection Staff perform 
collection services for its Magistrate Court, and the County is willing to provide such services 
upon the terms set forth herein; and 
 
WHEREAS, each Municipality has likewise requested that the County, through its County 
Attorney and Public Defender, provide criminal prosecution and defense services in criminal 
misdemeanor cases filed by the Municipality’s police officers in the Justice Court, and the County 
is willing to provide such services upon the terms set forth herein. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties hereby agree as follows: 
 
1. PURPOSE: 
 
A. The purpose of this IGA is to achieve greater efficiency in the operation of the 
Magistrate and Justice Courts by having the County Court Collection staff perform 
collection services for the Magistrate Courts.

<!-- page 364 -->
B. The further purpose of this IGA is to continue the practice of having Municipal police 
officers and other law enforcement officers file criminal misdemeanor cases, other than 
municipal code violations, in the appropriate Justice Court  for prosecution by the 
County Attorney and defense by the Public Defender, and to provide for compensation 
from each Municipality to the County to help defray the costs associated with these 
criminal justice services. 
 
2. TERM / EFFECTIVE DATE: The term of this IGA shall be one year from and after the 
Effective Date, unless terminated in accordance with paragraph 5 below. 
 
3. RESPONSIBILITIES OF THE COUNTY: 
 
A. The County Court Collection staff will provide appropriate collection services for 
civil and criminal fines, restitution, and other monetary amounts  ordered by each 
Municipality’s Magistrate Court. 
 
B. The County Court Collection staff will devote the same care and attention to 
Magistrate Court cases as to Justice Court cases. The County does not warrant that 
the services provided hereunder will be error -free, and the County shall have no 
liability to a Municipality in connection with collection services except in instances 
of gross negligence or willful misconduct. The County shall indemnify, defend, and 
hold harmless each municipality and its officials, officers, agents, and employees 
from and against any and all claims, actions, expenses, losses, or liabilities arising 
out of the County’s gross negligence or willful misconduct in relation to collection 
services. 
 
C. The County Attorney shall prosecute all criminal misdemeanor violations of state 
law filed in the Justice Courts by Municipal police officers  and other law 
enforcement officers. The Public Defender shall provide defense services as 
appointed to do so. Title 28 civil traffic cases, as well as all cases arising out of 
municipal code violations, shall be filed in the appropriate Magistrate Court unless 
otherwise agreed by the parties through their prosecuting attorneys. The County 
shall bear the burden and expense of providing all prosecution, defense, and support 
services required under this paragraph, subject to payment of an annual fee by each 
Municipality as set forth in paragraph 4 below.  
 
4. RESPONSIBILITIES OF EACH MUNICIPALITY: 
 
A. Court Collection and Criminal Justice Service Fee : As compensation in full to the 
County for all services to be provided hereunder, each Municipality shall pay an annual 
Court Collection and Criminal Justice Services Fee (the “Fee”) as follows:

<!-- page 365 -->
Municipality Annual Fee (FY 2025-2026) 
Holbrook $56,217.00 
Pinetop-Lakeside $16,570.00 
Show Low $40,463.00 
Snowflake $9,455.00 
Taylor $9,455.00 
Winslow $64,705.00 
 
(1) These fees shall  remain unchanged for the term of this Agreement. The parties 
acknowledge that this Fee structure is intended as an interim measure for this one-
year term, and future agreements may adopt a different methodology of calculating 
the Fee. 
 
B. Each Municipality shall provide, at no cost t o the County, timely and complete 
information and materials in relation to Magistrate Court cases as may reasonably be 
required for the County Court Collection staff to provide services hereunder.  
 
C. Each Municipality shall indemnify, defend, and hold harmless the County and its 
officials, officers, agents, and employees from and against any and all claims, actions, 
expenses, losses, or liabilities arising out of collection services provided by the County, 
except for matters within the scope  of the County’s indemnity to the Municipality as 
set forth in paragraph 3. B. 
 
5. WITHDRAWAL / TERMINATION:  
 
A. Any Municipality may withdraw from this IGA, effective as of the next anniversary of 
the Effective Date, by giving written notice to the County Manager no later than 120 
days before the next anniversary of the effective date; any such withdrawal  shall not 
have the effect of terminating this IGA so long as one or more other Municipalities 
remain as p arties. The County may terminate this IGA , effective as of the next 
anniversary of the Effective Date, by giving written notice  to the respective City or 
Town Managers of the Municipalities no later than 120 days before the next anniversary 
of the Effective Date. In the event of a withdrawal or termination, the parties shall 
cooperate to ensure a smooth transition so that the operations of their respective courts 
are not disrupted.  
 
6. AUTHORITY: Each party warrants and represents that the official executing this IGA on 
its behalf has been authorized to do so by duly adopted resolution of its governing board 
or council and that this IGA has been reviewed by its legal counsel and determined to be 
in the proper form and within the scope of its authority.   
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the duly authorized representatives of the parties have executed this 
IGA as of the Effective Date.

Board of Supervisors Regular 7. a.        
Meeting Date: 11/18/2025  
Title: Navajo County Rural Diversion Program
Submitted For: Brad Carlyon, County Attorney  Submitted By: Lynda Young, Office Manager
Department: County Attorney
Presented By: Brad Carlyon
Subject:
Presentation of the Navajo County Rural Diversion Program.  
Background: 
This presentation will provide an update on Navajo County's Rural Diversion Program.  This program is
an alternative method of holding low level offenders, whose offense has a nexus to substance abuse,
accountable for violations of the law through participation in an evidence-based education or treatment
program that targets factors related to recidivism. The Program effectively redirected limited criminal
justice resources, brought program participant’s rehabilitation, and gave rise to safer communities. The
Program diverted criminal cases from formal court proceedings and allowed for the dismissal of charges
when the Program successfully completed. 
Attachments
FY26 Navajo County Diversion Program Presentation 
Form Review

<!-- page 401 -->
Navajo County Attorney’s Office 
Court Diversion Program
Clinical Supervision provided by Spectrum Healthcare Group
Financial Supervision provided by Polara Health
Managed Care Coordination provided by Erin Eshkibok
Care Management provided by Cynthia Jensen, Kirk Benally and Andrew Eshkibok

<!-- page 402 -->
MISSION
The Navajo County Attorney’s Office has established a
Court Diversion Program to provide an alternative method
of holding low level offenders, whose offense often has a
nexus to substance abuse, or mental health conditions
accountable for violations of the law through participation
in an evidence-based education or treatment program
that targets factors related to recidivism. The Program
effectively redirects limited criminal justice resources, brings
about participants’ rehabilitation, and gives rise to safer
communities. The Program diverts criminal cases from
formal court proceedings and allows for the dismissal of
charges if the Program is successfully completed.

<!-- page 403 -->
Careful screening process and case planning
- Behavioral health screening and referrals
- Social determinants of health screening
- Two methods of random drug and alcohol
testing
- Weekly or more contact with care manager
- GPS monitoring and electronic check-ins.
- Group attendance
- Remote services and in-person services

<!-- page 404 -->

the program (93 people)

• Indicator of other social determinant

Social Determinants of Health

<!-- page 409 -->
Social Determinants of Health Factors That Correlate to a
Higher Risk to Re-offend
 Food insecure- nearly 40% of participants
 Homeless- 11% of participants
 History of trauma- 66% of participants
 Under or unemployed- 58% of participants
 Family history of substance abuse- 67% of participants

<!-- page 410 -->
Of the 93 people who have successfully completed the program,
they collectively parented 128 children…
….128 children whose parents are more likely to be
employed without a criminal record, who have achieved
and maintained sobriety, who have been introduced to
valuable services to improve the family’s quality of life and
children whose parents have been prompted to address
their trauma- all aimed to stop the cycle.

<!-- page 411 -->
Delayne
Before
• History of trauma
• Family history of substance abuse
• Unemployed
• Food insecure
• No plans for continued education
• Never gone to treatment or AA
• Two referrals to diversion- multiple arrests for underage
drinking
• Difficult family relationships

<!-- page 412 -->
After successfully completing diversion
• Achieved and maintained sobriety
• Completed intensive outpatient treatment and continues relapse
prevention treatment by her own choice
• Goes to AA with her mom
• Improved relationship with parents, helping care for father while he is in
hospice care.
• Completed college entrance exams
• Got a job
• Has not returned to drinking

<!-- page 413 -->
“I want to be sober because I want to become a
better me. I have a long life ahead of me yet and
I still want to go to school. Plus, I don't want this
ruining the relationship between me & my parents.
Another thing is I don't want to be out in jail
anymore or end up doing time. I want to find a
job & stay away from alcohol or stop hanging
around the wrong people. Friends who aren't my
friends. I'm realizing what I'm doing to myself and
what harm it could bring me.”
-Delayne

<!-- page 414 -->
Kelly
 Family history of substance abuse and mental health concerns
 Two prior suicide attempts
 Used alcohol the first time at age 11 for the purpose of getting
drunk
 Unemployed
 History of trauma

<!-- page 415 -->
Kelly
 Improved relationship with family
 Participated in therapeutic interventions throughout program
and after stabilizing her mood and anger responses.
 Achieved and maintained sobriety
 Left abusive relationship

<!-- page 416 -->
Kelly
Did the diversion program help you?
“Yes, TREMENDOUSLY!  I lost my father this morning and
if it weren't for this program I wouldn't have been able
to repair my relationship with him.  Because of CCP I
was able to care for him, transport him, get to know him
and connect with him on an emotional level.  I got my
father back and he got his
daughter back. This program is a GOD send.”

<!-- page 417 -->
Keshaun
Did the diversion program help you?
“Yes, I work full-time and now hold a
permanent job doing what I've always
wanted to do.  I keep from toxic and
negative influences.  I've been all over
the western US helping fight fires.  This is
what you've given me.”

<!-- page 418 -->
“Yeah, It kept me in check long enough to
change everything I was doing.”
-Novalee
Did the diversion program help you?
“Yes, the program helped me
wake up and realize what I was
doing to myself.”
-Rowan
“Yes, I don't drink anymore. My wife
sat in on the diversion zoom groups
and found it also gave her an
awakening.  We both have
changed for the better.”
-Ryan
“Yes, I feel like I'm clearing of
mind and can help my father
and sister.  I still go to
ChangePoint and love myself.”
-Skylar
Yes, I was able to spend the rest of my
grandma's days with her.  This program
gave me the clarity to be there with her.
I know I still need help, and my journey is
not over but if it weren't for this program I
wouldn't have even gotten started or
know what it was like to be clear
minded.  I still struggle but I know when
and how to find help.
-Tina
Yes, I was able to leave the
toxic environment.  I
relocated to the valley.
-Shytalia (3-time participant)

Board of Supervisors Regular 8. a.        
Meeting Date: 11/18/2025  
Title: FP-A 25-001
Submitted For: Katherine Wright, Planner II  Submitted By: Katherine Wright, Planner II
Department: Planning & Development Services 
Presented By: Katherine Wright
Motion before the Board:
Public Hearing: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 29-2025, approving/denying a Final
Plat Amendment to allow two lots (029 and 030) to be absorbed into common area (Tract A).
Background:
June 21, 1999 the Board of Supervisors approved the Final Plat for The Village in Resolution No.
58-99.  Lots 029 and 030 were never developed.  If approved, the proposed project will combine lots 029
and 030 into common ground, Tract A.
 
Recommendation: 
Navajo County Planning and Development Services Staff recommend approval of the Final Plat
Amendment.

BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Hearing Date: November 18 th, 2025
Case No: FP-A-25-001
Action: Final Plat Amendment
Applicant/Owner: Chip Yetzer
Project Name: The Village
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
Recommendation: Staff recommends approval of the Final Plat Amendment.
Location: The property is located in the Pinetop-Lakeside area, on APN# 411-90-129 and 411-
90-130.
Size: Two (2) parcels totaling .04 acres.
Supervisor District: V.
Zoning: SD.
Property Owners: Village Home Owners Association.
Applicant: Chip Yetzer.
Representative: Chip Yetzer.
REQUEST DESCRIPTION:
A request for a Final Plat Amendment to allow two lots (029 and 030) to be absorbed into
common area (Tract A). Owner: Village Home Owners Association. Agent: Chip Yetzer.
GENERAL CHARACTER OF NEIGHBORHOOD:

<!-- page 437 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e2 | 
The subject property is located in the Pinetop-Lakeside area.  Adjoining this property to the
north is Stone Pine Estates Unit II.  To the east and south of this property is Pinetop Lakes
Sports Village #4.    To the west is High Country Village Condominiums.  Properties in this
subdivision are a mixture of single and multiple-family zones, with a variety of single and
multiple-family residences.
BACKGROUND/HISTORY:
June 21, 1999 the Board of Supervisors approved the Final Plat for The Village in Resolution
No. 58-99.
PROJECT DESCRIPTION:
If approved, the proposed project will combine lots 029 and 030 into common ground, Tract
A.
ZONING DISTRICT:
ARTICLE 9 – SPECIAL DEVELOPMENT ZONE (SD)
Section 901 – Purpose
1. The purpose of the Special Development Zone is to provide for the development of
subdivision and other land use projects containing various dwelling types, necessary
commercial centers, off-street parking areas, open space areas for recreation, and to preserve
a rural atmosphere.
2. Deviations from design standards for the specific zone or zones may be permitted provided
open spaces are developed and maintained for the use of the residents and provided further
that the parcel size, soil conditions, terrain, ground cover and other significant natural
features favor this type of development.
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN/AREA PLAN:
Comprehensive Plan
Land Use Goals and Policies
Land Use Policy #16
Navajo County supports development that provides buffers between different land uses, such as
increased setbacks, walls, fences, and landscaping.
 The project will be absorbing unused lots into Tract A.
Analysis:
This request for a Final Plat Amendment meets the goals and policies of the Navajo County
Comprehensive Plan, as described in this Staff Report.  This proposal would allow for

<!-- page 438 -->
Board of Supervisors          P a g e3 | 
alternative usage on property that is not being utilized.
Planning and Development Services Staff has received zero (0) public comment for the project
as of November 6th, 2025.
FINDINGS OF FACT:
1. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan.
The proposed project meets all applicable goals within Navajo County’s Comprehensive
Plan, as noted within this report.
2. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning
district in which the property is located.
The proposed use is compatible with permissible uses within the Special Development (SD)
zoning districts.
3. The proposed use is compatible with the current and likely future uses of
properties in the vicinity of the proposed use.
The proposed use has been determined to be compatible with current and likely future uses
per the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance.
4. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health,
safety, and general welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use.
Given the nature of the property and the zoning in the area, there should be minimal
adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general welfare of properties in the vicinity
of the proposed use.

RESOLUTION ____-25
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS DENYING
A FINAL PLAT AMENDMENT FOR THE VILLAGE SUBDIVISION
WHEREAS, an application for a Final Plat Amendment was duly filed on September
29th, 2025 by Chip Yetzer to allow a two lots (029 and 030) to be absorbed into common area
(Tract A); and
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County:
APN # 411-90-129 and 411-90-130, Township 08N, Range 23E, Section 03 of the Gila and Salt
River Base and Meridian; and
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of Staff, found that
the Final Plat Amendment is not consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare and
should be denied; and
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED the Board of Supervisors hereby deny
the Final Plat Amendment.

Board of Supervisors Regular 9. a.        
Meeting Date: 11/18/2025  
Title: Delivery and Application of Magnesium Chloride, Magnesium Chloride/Lignin Blend and
Lignin B25-08-022
Submitted For: Madhav Mundle, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Alexandria Porras, Administrative Specialist
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Madhav Mundle
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of Contract Number B25-08-022 Delivery and Application of Magnesium
Chloride, Magnesium Chloride/Lignin Blend and Lignin to Desert Mountain Corporation.
Background:
Public Works is utilizing subgrade stabilizers (Magnesium Chloride/Lignin Blend and Lignin) to stabilize
and reduce the lifecycle cost of maintenance on unpaved roads within the County.  
Sealed bids were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Governmental Center,
Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 PM M.S.T., October 1, 2025 in strict accordance with the specifications on
file in the office of the Navajo County Department of Public Works. No bids were accepted after 3:00 PM
M.S.T. The bids were publicly opened and read aloud at 3:15 PM. Only one bid was received by Desert
Mountain Corporation.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends award of contract B25-08-022 Delivery and Application of Magnesium Chloride,
Magnesium Chloride/Lignin Blend and Lignin to Desert Mountain Corporation.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY 25/26 
Budgeted Y/N: Y 
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
Funds are budgeted for FY 2026.

THIS CONTRACT is made and entered into as of November 18, 2025 (the “Effective Date”) 
by and between Navajo County (“County”) and Desert Mountain Corporation 
(“Contractor”). 
 
1. SCOPE OF WORK: Contractor shall furnish any and all materials, equipment and labor 
r e q u i r e d  f o r  p e r f o r m i n g  a l l  w o r k  f o r  D e l i v e ry  a n d  Ap p l i c a t i o n  o f  M a g n e s i u m  C h l o r i d e ,  
Magnesium Chloride/Lignin Blend, and Lignin, Contract Number B25-08-022, in 
accordance with the Contract Documents, in a workmanlike manner to the satisfaction 
of County through its Engineers. 
2. CONTRACT DOCUMENTS: The Contract Documents comprise this Contract and the 
attached ☒ Notice of Invitation for Bids, ☒ Speciﬁcations, ☐ General Provisions, ☒ 
Special Provisions, ☒ Proposal dated September 29, 2025 ☒ Bid Schedule, ☒ Bid Bond, 
☐ Performance Bond, ☒ Affidavit of Non -Collusion, ☒ Subcontracting Certiﬁcation, ☐ 
Plans, and addendums thereto, if any.  If there is any conﬂict in the documents, this 
Contract shall control over all others; the Speciﬁcations shall control over the General 
Provisions and Special Provisions; and the Special Provisions shall control ov er the 
General Provisions. 
3. NO LIENS / TIME FOR COMPLETION: Contractor shall furnish all materials, equipment 
and labor in accordance with the Contract Documents free and clear of all claims, liens 
and charges whatsoever, within the time, or times, stated in the Proposal. 
4. CANCELLATION: This Contract is subject to cancellation pursuant to A.R.S. § 38 -511, 
(concerning conﬂicts of interest). 
5. PAYMENTS:  Payment shall be made on the basis of the unit price(s) bid for the awarded 
items in the Bid Schedule for Contract Number B25-08 -022.  Contractor shall submit 
invoices to County for all work performed hereunder .  No later than 30 days after each 
invoice has been veriﬁed and approved by the Engineer, County shall pay to Contractor 
100% of the approved amount. 
6. CERTIFICATIONS: 
A. PROHIBITION OF BOYCOTT OF ISRAEL:  Contractor certiﬁes that neither it 
nor its subcontractors are currently or will be engaged in, for the duration of 
this Contract engage in, a boycott of Israel as deﬁned by A.R.S. § 35-393 et 
seq.  Violation of this certiﬁcation may result in an action by the County up to 
and including termination of the Contract. 
B. WRITTEN CERTIFICATION PURSUANT TO A.R.S. §35-394:  Pursuant to 
A.R.S. § 35-394, Contractor certiﬁes that it is not currently using, and agrees

<!-- page 452 -->
for the duration of this Agreement to not use (1) the forced labor of ethnic 
Uyghurs in the People’s Republic of China; (2) any goods or services 
produced by the forced labor of ethnic Uyghurs in the People’s Republic of 
China; and (3) any contractors, subcontractors, or suppliers that use the 
forced labor of ethnic Uyghurs in the People’s Republic of China.  The 
Subrecipient further acknowledges and agrees that (1) if the Subrecipient 
becomes aware during the term of this Agreement that it is not in compliance 
with this certiﬁcation that the Subrecipient will notify the County within ﬁve 
(5) business days after becoming aware of the noncompliance; and (2) if the 
Subrecipient does not provide the County with a written certiﬁcation that the 
Subrecipient has remedied the noncompliance within one hundred eighty 
(180) after giving notice thereof, the same shall constitute a material breach 
of this Agreement, which shall then terminate automatically, except that if 
the Agreement termination date occurs before the end of the remedy period, 
this Agreement terminates on the Agreement’s termination date.  The County 
retains the legal right to inspect the records of the Contractor and all 
Subcontractors to ensure compliance with this certiﬁcation for the duration 
of this Agreement.

Board of Supervisors Regular 9. b. 1. a.        
Meeting Date: 11/18/2025  
Title: Kimley-Horn - Woodruff Drainage Evaluation Phase 2 Amendment No. 1 - Dist III
Submitted For: William Flake, Assistant County Engineer 
Submitted By: Kathleen Outland, Procurement Officer
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Contract Amendment Number 1 to the Kimley-Horn and Associates contract for the Woodruff Drainage
Evaluation Phase 2 Project extends the contract through March 31, 2026
Background:
On December 10, 2024, the Board of Supervisors approved a Professional Services Contract with
Kimley-Horn to provide evaluation services to the Woodruff Drainage project. Woodruff is an
unincorporated area in Navajo County (County) that experiences frequent nuisance flooding, mostly along
1st Street and County Road, due to the lack of existing drainage infrastructure. Currently, runoff flows
along roadway alignments in undersized roadside ditches or adjacent properties, and ponds at
intersections. Amendment Number 1 extends this contract through March 31, 2026.
